ulaka makkaḷ toki vivaraṅkaḷ palvēṟu nūlkaḷiliruntu eṭukkappaṭṭavi. ki.mu.500, ki.pi.1, ki.pi. 1600 ākiya āṇṭukaḷil intiya makkaḷ toki vivaraṅkaḷ rōmilā tāpar eḻutiya paṇṭiya intiyāviṉ peṅkuyiṉ varalāṟu (2002) puttakattiṟ kaṇṭavi.

2. mekarkār eṉṉum iṭattil ceyta akaḻvāykaḷukkup piṉ, cintu nākarikattiṉ muntiya toṭakka nilikkāṉa cāṉṟukaḷ ki.mu.7000 liruntu toṭarcciyākak kiṭikkiṉṟaṉa. cintu nākarikam intiyāviṟkuḷḷēyē tōṉṟi iṭiyīṭiṉṟip paṭippaṭiyāka mutircci peṟṟu vēta kālattukku (ki.mu.1500-) vekumuṉṉar iruntē nilipeṟṟu iruntatu.

3. āṭu māṭu vaḷarttal, tavacaṅkaḷ payiriṭutal, parutti payiriṭutalpōṉṟa tuṟikaḷil cintu nākarikattiṉ tolvaravukkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷi mekarkār āyvu veḷikkoṇarntuḷḷatu vēḷāṇmiyē nāṭṭuppuṟa makkaḷiṉ perun toḻilāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum. āṭu māṭu mēyttuk koṇṭu avvap poḻutu kuṭipeyarntu vāḻntu vantavarkaḷum (pastoral nomads and cattle keeping tribes) iruntiruppar. cintup pakutiyil ki.mu.3000il irunta taṭpaveppa nili iṉṟu uḷḷatu pōltāṉ iruntirukkum eṉpar pocel (2002:15 the climate of this region was not markedly different in the third millennium B.C. from the one we have today”. 

4. kalipaṅkaṉil 5000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar uḻutu pātti kaṭṭiya amippu (toṭakkakālac cintu nākarikattic cārntatu) kiṭittuḷḷatu kuṟippiṭattakkatu. kōtumiyum pārliyum mukkiyap payirkaḷākavum, uṇavup poruḷkaḷākavum iruntaṉa. cōḷam, kampu, kēḻvarakum payirāyiṉa. lōtalil nel umi akaḻvāyvil kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭatu. ulakilēyē mutalil paruttit tuṇi uṟpatti ceyta nāṭu intiyāvē. mekarkāril parutti ki.mu.5000 kku muṉṉarē payaṉpaṭuttappaṭṭuḷḷatu. (cumēriyāvil piṉṉar paruttit tuṇikku ‘cintu’ eṉṟē peyar vaḻaṅkiyatu) kirāmap puṟaṅkaḷil vēḷāṇmi ceḻippāka iruntamiyiṉāltāṉ mokañcotarō, hārappā mutaliya peru nakaraṅkaḷukkut tēviyāṉa tavacaṅkaḷ taṭiyiṉṟik kiṭittaṉa. mokañcotarō, hārappā nakaraṅkaḷil kaṇṭupiṭitta cila kaṭṭaṭa eccaṅkaḷ muṉṉar tāṉiyak kaḷañciyaṅkaḷ eṉak kūṟap paṭṭu vantaṉa. āṉāl aṇmikkālak karuttu avi pala poruḷkaḷiyum vikkum kiṭaṅkukaḷ (warehouses) tām, tāṉiyak kaḷañciyaṅkaḷ alla eṉpatākum (pocel: 2002. pak 247)

5. kutiri tavira iṉṟu cintup pakutiyil uḷḷa aṉittu vilaṅkukaḷum aṉṟum iruntaṉa. iṉṟu appakutiyil illāta yāṉikaḷum kāṇṭāmirukaṅkaḷumkūṭa aṉṟu cintup pakutiyil iruntaṉa.

6. (i) kaṉimap poruḷkaḷil irumpu tavira ēṉiya paṇṭi māḻikaḷiṉāl āṉa poruṭkaḷ (veṇkala aram, kōṭari, ampu muṉikaḷ; īṭṭimuṉikaḷ, ūcikaḷ, ēṉaṅkaḷ) kiṭittuḷḷaṉa. irumpum urukkum teṉṉintiyāvilum kiḻakku intiyāvilum tāṉ ulakilēyē mutaṉ mutalil irumput tātuviliruntu uṟpatti ceyyappaṭṭaviyākat tōṉṟukiṉṟaṉa. eṉiṉum cintuveḷi nākarika muṭivu kālamākiya ki.mu.1800 aḷavil aṅku irumpu kuṟippiṭattakka aḷavukkup paravavilli. ataṟkup piṉṉar intiyāviṟkuḷ nuḻinta āriyarkaḷum irumpik koṇṭu vantavarkaḷ allar. ‘āriyarkaḷ irumpuk karuvikaḷuṭaṉ vantaṉar. eṉavē eḷitil veṉṟaṉar’. eṉpatu kaṟpaṉi eṉṟu kārṭaṉ kūṟukiṟār. (D.H. Gordon: The Prehistoric background of Indian culture; 1960) rik vētam ‘ayam’ eṉak kūṟuvatu potuvāka cempu pōṉṟa māḻiyiyē; irumpi alla. tamiḻil poṉ eṉpatu māḻikkup potup peyarāka vaḻaṅkiyataṉṟō. [irum (karuppu) + poṉ - irumpoṉ - irumpu] ceppuk karuvikaḷōṭu kaṟ karuvikaḷiyum toṭarntu payaṉpaṭuttiṉar cintu veḷiyiṉar.

(ii) akaḻvāyvil kiṭitta cāṉṟukaḷiliruntu cempu, veṇkalap poruḷ kammiyar, kaṟkaruvikaḷil ceyta kaṭṭaṭa vēlikkārar, muttiri ceyyunar pōṉṟa palavakit toḻil viṟpaṉṉarkaḷ aṉṟu iruntami teḷivākiṟatu.

(iii) ti. cattiyamūrtti (2009) kaṭṭuri Indus to Tamaraparni – il ceppukkalavi (Copper alloy) payaṉpāṭṭil mokañcotarōvukkum āticcanallūrukkum irunta oru oppumiyic cuṭṭukiṟār. cempil 1% ārceṉik - ai vēṇṭumeṉṟē kalantu ak kalaviyiṉ vaṉmiyi (hardness) 124 VHN liruntu 177VHN atikarittuḷḷaṉar. intiyāvil toṉmic ceppukkalavi ēṉaṅkaḷil mokeñcotarōvilum, āticcanallūrilum kiṭittavaṟṟil maṭṭumē ārceṉik 4% m, ataṟku mēlum uḷḷatu. āticca nallūr, mokañcotarō nākarikaṅkaḷiṉ orumippāṭṭukkāṉa cāṉṟukaḷil oṉṟāka itiyum karutalāmeṉpār avar.

7. (i) cintu nākarikap perunakara makkaḷ ceḻittu vāḻntataṟku vāṇikamē aṭippaṭi. uḷnāṭṭu vāṇikattil intiyāviṉ pala pakutikaḷiliruntum koṇarnta vilimatippuḷḷap poruṭkaḷ paṅkēṟṟaṉa. amecāṉ eṉṉum paccikkal nīlakirip pakutiyiliruntum, caṅku, muttu mutaliyavi pāṇṭiya nāṭṭiṉiṉṟum koṇṭu cellappaṭṭirukkalām. kaṭal vaḻiyākavum, āṟukaḷ vaḻiyākavum poruḷkaḷ paṭakukaḷil aṉuppappaṭṭatuṭaṉ, uḷnāṭṭil erutukaḷiyum kaḻutikaḷiyum vaṇikac cāttu vilaṅkukaḷākap payaṉpaṭuttiyiruppar. malip pakutikaḷiliruntu cakki mukkik kal (flint) rājastāṉ pōṉṟa pakutikaḷiliruntu cemputtātu, mahārāṣṭiratti liruntu cevvantikkal, curāṣṭirattiliruntu akēṭ ((Agate) kaṟkaḷ; karunāṭakattiliruntu taṅkam pōṉṟavi uḷnāṭṭu vāṇikap poruḷkaḷil cilavākum.

(ii) cintu nākarikap pakutikaḷililēyē ciṟanta pācimaṇikaḷ uṟpatti ceyyappaṭṭaṉa. 1200 ṭikiri celciyas varic cūṭēṟṟi maṇikaḷit tayārikkum ulikaḷ kalipaṅkaṉil kiṭittuḷḷaṉa.

8. ayalnāṭukaḷōṭu, kuṟippāka mecapotāmiyāvuṭaṉ perumaḷavu vāṇikam naṭantuḷḷatu. mecapotāmiyāvil cumār 24 hārappā muttirikaḷ kiṭittuḷḷaṉa. mecapotāmiya ciliṇṭar muttirikaḷ cila mokeñcotarōvil kiṭittuḷḷaṉa. mecapo tāmiya cācaṉaṅkaḷil melukā (Meluhha) eṉṉum nāṭṭiliruntu karuṅkāli maram, rattiṉak kaṟkaḷ, tantam pōṉṟavi iṟakkumati ceyyappaṭṭatākak kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. melukā eṉpatu cintu veḷiyit tāṉ kuṟittatu eṉpati aṉivarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. (parpōlā, 1975; rōmilā tāpar 1975, ālciṉ 1988, leviṭ 2009) mecapotāmiyā vukku paruttit tuṇi perumaḷavukku ēṟṟumatiyāki yirukkavēṇṭum. vāṇikattil (veḷināṭu maṟṟum uḷnāṭu) cippaṅkaḷik kaṭṭi muttirikaḷ iṭṭaṉar. āyirakkaṇakkāṉa muttirikaḷ eḻuttukaḷuṭaṉ kiṭittuḷḷaṉa. ivipaṟṟi piṉ iyalil kāṇka. vāṇikat tiṟkāka rattiṉak kaṟkaḷ kiṭṭum (vaṭakiḻakku āpkāṉistāṉil uḷḷa) ṣōrttukay (Shortugi) eṉṉum iṭattil cintu veḷi nākarika makkaḷ (vaṇikar?) kuṭiyiruppu iruntatu akaḻvāyvil kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. cintuveḷi nākarika eṭi aḷavukaḷ tamiḻariṭiyē vaḻaṅkiyaviyē. iti piṉvarum iyalil kāṇka.

9. cintu nākarikattukkum elām, cumēriya nākarikaṅkaḷukkum iṭiyil mika neruṅkiya toṭarpu ki.mu.4000 ttiliruntē iruntu vantatu eṉpati mārṣal mutal pocel vari aṉivarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar [eṉiṉum tirāviṭa moḻipēcunar ēṟṟattil Dravidian Ascent  teṟkiliruntu ceṉṟa tirāviṭarkaḷ tām cintuppakutiyil ciṟanta nākarikattit tōṟṟuvittup piṉṉar elām cumēriya nākarikaṅkaḷiyum uruvākkiṉar eṉṉum hīrās pōṉṟavar kaḷuṭiya karuttu iṉṟiya variyil ittuṟiyil valla paṉṉāṭ ṭaṟiñar perumpālōr ēṟpiṉip peṟavilli] āyiṉum am mūṉṟu nākarikaṅ kaḷukkum iṭiyilāṉa vāṇikat toṭarpu perum pālum mēṭṭukkuṭiyiṉar payaṉpaṭuttum āṭamparap poruḷkaḷ (rattiṉak kaṟkaḷ, kalipporuḷkaḷ, māḻip poruṭkaḷ, paruttit tuṇi mutaliyavi) cārntatākavē iruntatu. ki.mu.2000 iliruntu orupāl im mūṉṟu nākarikaṅkaḷukkum, maṟupāl avaṟṟukku vaṭakkil irunta pākṭiriyāṉā mārjiyāṉā tolliyal paṇpāṭṭu (pā.mā.to.-BMAC) nākarikattukkum vāṇikattoṭarpu iruntirukkum eṉṟum, mokañcotarōviṉ iṟuti nūṟu, irunūṟu āṇṭukaḷil pā.mā.to. paṇpāṭṭu makkaḷ cintu nākarikap perumparappiṟ kuḷḷum varattoṭaṅkiyirukkalām eṉṟum pocel (2002:235) karutukiṟār.

10. cintu muttirikaḷil oṉṟu vilaṅkukaḷ cūḻa irukkum civaṉik (pacupatiyik) kāṭṭuvatākak koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. vaṭa airōppāvil cermaṉikku vaṭakkē ṭeṉmārk nāṭṭil kaṇtastirap eṉṉum iṭattil 28-5-1891 il kaṇṭeṭukkappaṭṭa 21/4aṭi kuṟukkaḷavuḷḷa veḷḷik koppariyiṉ mēlē poṟikkappaṭṭuḷḷa uruvaṅkaḷil anta pacupati pōṉṟa uruvamum uḷḷatu. akkoppari ki.mu.200il ceyyappaṭṭatu. cintu veḷik kāla poṉ - veḷḷik kaliñarkaḷ piyappiya mēli nāṭukaḷilum paraviyiruntataṉ kāraṇamākat tāṉ avvuruvam ap pāttirattil poṟikkappaṭa nērntatu eṉpati viḷakkum ‘kaṇtastirap koppari’ eṉṉum kaṭṭuriyicayaṉṭipik amerikkaṉ 1992 mārc itaḻil timōtti ṭēlar eḻutiyuḷḷār.

11(i) cintuveḷi makkaḷ vīṭukaḷil ammiyum kuḻaviyum, uralum payaṉpaṭuttiṉar. maṭpāṇṭak kali ciṟappāka iruntatu. pāṉikaḷil cittira vēlippāṭu iruntatu. pala tuḷikaḷ iṭṭa kaṉaṟcaṭṭi payaṉ paṭuttiṉar. vīṭukaḷil tāṉiyaṅkaḷik koṭṭi vikkat tāḻikaḷi (kutirkaḷi) payaṉpaṭuttiṉar. kuḻantikaḷiṉ viḷiyāṭṭup poruḷkaḷ (paṟavi vaṭivil ūtu kuḻalkaḷ; tali acikkum erutukaḷ; kōlikaḷ) ērāḷamākak kiṭittuḷḷaṉa.

(ii) kaḷimaṇṇāṟ ceyta pommi vaṇṭikaḷ cila kiṭittuḷḷaṉa. ārakkāl illāta cakkaraṅkaḷik koṇṭavi. itē pōṉṟa vaṇṭikaḷ iṉṟum cintu mānilattil uḷḷaṉa.

(iii) ammakkaḷ poṉ, veḷḷi, veṇpoṉ (Electrum) cempu, veṇkalam, tantam, elumpu, mākkal, irattiṉak kaṟkaḷ, cuṭṭakaḷimaṇ, caṅku mutaliyavaṟṟik koṇṭu tattam takutikkēṟṟa aṇikaḷi vitam vitamākac ceytu aṇintu vantaṉar. kiṭittuḷḷa aḻakiya aṇikalaṉkaḷil cila: kaḻuttu māli, oṭṭiyāṇam, āṟu carakki
yaṇi, arakku uḷḷīṭu koṇṭa kāppu, palavaki vaḷiyal, kāl kāppu (taṇṭi), neṟṟic cuṭṭi, kātaṇi, mōtiram, pottāṉ, talināṭā, koṇṭi ūci pōṉṟavi ākum.

12. intiyāviṉ taṟkālak kali, toḻil nuṭpaviyal tuṟikaḷ palavaṟṟilum cintu nākarikak kāla vērkaḷik kāṇalām eṉpār ṭi.pi. akarvāl (2009, Harappan Technology and its legacy).. palatuṟikaḷilum am māperum cintu nākarikap pakuti muḻuvatilum eytiyirunta toḻilnuṭpac cātaṉinili paṇṭiya ulaka nākarikam etilum kāṇātatu eṉalām. (ālciṉ: “the technical level achieved over so great an area as is demonstrated in the Harappan civilisation is probably unique in the ancient world”). kaṟpaṉivaḷamikka kalip paṭippukkaḷi mikutiyākak kāṇa iyalavilli eṉpati ālciṉ cuṭṭik kāṭṭiviṭṭu “oru vēḷi avarkaḷ taṅkaḷ kaṟpaṉik kalit tiṟaṉik kāṭṭum cātaṉikaḷit tuṇikaḷ āṭikaḷ pōṉṟa tuṟikaḷil kāṭṭiyirukkalām. (avi namakku iṉṟu kiṭṭavilli)” eṉṟu karutukiṟār.

kali vaḷarcci

13. hārappāvil kiṭitta 10 ce.mī. uyarac cevvantikkal Red Jasper āṭavaṉ cili (kaḻuttiliruntu toṭivari) ki.mu. 2200 - 1900 kālattatu, (tali, kikaḷi iṇippataṟkāṉa tuḷikaḷum uḷḷaṉa.)

14. mokañcotarō DK 1909 pakutiyil kiṭṭiya cuṇṇāmpuk kallil cetukkiya pūcāri - aracaṉ Priest - King’ 18 ce.mī. paṭivamum kuṟippiṭattakkatu, atil cittirap pū vēli ceyta pōrvi, oḻuṅkāṉa tāṭi, taliyil muṭiyic cīvip paṭṭi kaṭṭiyuḷḷatu ākiyavaṟṟik kāṇkiṟōm. atu pūcāriyumalla, aracaṉumalla, kaṟpaṉi vaṭivamē eṉpar pocel.

15. veṇkalattāṟ ceyyappaṭṭa (11 ce.mī. uyara) naṭaṉamātiṉ ciliyum cintu veḷi naṭaṉak kalikkuc cāṉṟākum. harappāvil kiṭṭiya 10 ce.mī uyara cāmpal niṟakkaṟ cili oṉṟu uṇarcciyuṭaṉ naṭaṉam ceyya toṭaṅkuvatu pōla iṭatu kālit tūkki valakkāl mītu niṉṟu naṭaṉam āṭa niṟpatu pōlat) tōṉṟukiṉṟatu. piṟ kālattil tamiḻ nāṭṭil ki.pi.1000 ai oṭṭi āṭavallāṉukku (naṭarāca ṉukku) ciṟanta paṭimaṅkaḷ amikkappaṭṭaṉavaṉṟō avaṟṟukkāṉa tōṟṟuvāy cintu veḷiyilē ceyyappaṭṭirukkalāmō eṉpatu caṅkāliyā pōṉṟavarkaḷ karuttu.

16. intiya iciyiṉ varalāṟu eṉṉum nūlil ō. kōsvāmi icit tuṟiyilum āriyar nuḻinta kālattoṭakkattiliruntu āriyarukku muntiya (tamiḻ) icikkūṟukaḷ [aṉṟu tamiḻiyac cintu nākarikattilum avi iruntirukkum] perumaḷavil cērntuḷḷati viḷakkukiṟār. “intiya icikku cāmavētamē piṟappiṭam eṉṟa) kaṟpaṉik kūṟṟi muṉvittukkoṇṭu ici aṟivukkum payiṟcikkum āriyar tamakku muṉ attuṟiyil vallōrāka irunta āriyarallātāriṭam kaṭaṉpaṭṭiruppati maṟakkaṭikka muṭintuḷḷatu” eṉkiṟār. [By this fiction (that Saman is the source of all Indian music) alone it was possible to forget conveniently the debt the Aryans owed to the non – Aryans for their musical knowledge and practice.” The Story of Indian Music; 1957] 

cāmavēta icikkum, potuvāka intiya ici muḻumikkum, tamiḻiciyē aṭippaṭiyāṉatu eṉpati Tamil Culture  itaḻil 1958- 59l (VII-1 & VIII-3) eḻutiya kaṭṭurikaḷilum ‘tamiḻici tanta peru veḷḷam’ [1978: tamiḻaka nuṇkalikaḷ (tamiḻp paṇpāṭṭu maṉṟam / pāri niliyam, nūliṉ pak. 12-66] eṉṉum kaṭṭuriyilum ku. kōtaṇṭapāṇi piḷḷi ātāraṅkaḷuṭaṉ niṟuviyuḷḷār.

17. ki.mu.3200 - 2600 kālattiya toṭakkanili (Early Harappan) cintu nākarikamāṉatu nūṟu āṇṭukālam (2600 - 2500) vaḷarnilic cintu nākarikamāka urumāṟat toṭaṅkip piṉṉar ki.mu.2500 – 1900 (mottam 600 āṇṭukaḷ) vaḷarnili cintu Mature Harappan nākarika māka āṉatu evvāṟu nikaḻntirukkalām eṉpatu paṟṟiya pocel uṉṉippukaḷic (2002: 47-53, 55-58) curukkamākak kāṇalām. cintu nākarika makkaḷiṭam maṭṭumē viḷaṅkiya oru taṉi laṭciyak kōṭpāṭē (ideology)  vaḷarnilic cintu nākarikatti uruvākkiyatu. cintu nākarikattil maṭṭumē kāṇum piṉvarum nāṉku taṉmikaḷi yum uḷḷaṭakkiyatu ak kōṭpāṭu:-

paṇpāṭṭu muṟiyi valintu		their heritage and replace it with a new

18. antak kālakaṭṭattil (ki.mu.3000 - 2000) paṇṭiya nākarika nāṭukaḷil aracu State eṉpatu eppaṭi iruntirukkum eṉpatu paṟṟi tolliya lāḷar, varalāṟṟāciriyar pōṉṟa paltuṟi aṟiñarkaḷiṭam orumitta karuttu illi. eṉiṉum avvaraciṉ taṉmikaḷil piṉvaru vaṉavum iruntirukkum eṉṟu avarkaḷ karutukiṉṟaṉar:

periya aḷavu poruḷātāra, vaṇika naṭavaṭikkikaḷ; taṉittaṉik kittoḻilkaḷil viṟpaṉṉarkaḷ; niruvākap paṇiyik kavaṉittavarkaḷ (kaṇakkar, mēṟpārviyāḷar uṭpaṭa) potu makkaḷiṭiyē mēl / kīḻ pirivukaḷ (perumpālum aracar eṉa aḻikkappaṭṭa) talivar / talivarkaḷ – potumakkaḷik kaṭṭuppaṭutti oṟukkum atikārattuṭaṉ; at talivarkaḷukku periya mukkiyattuvam; aracu cārnta camayam, kōyilkaḷ paṟṟiya taṭayaṅkaḷ

ippaṭippaṭṭa kūṟukaḷik koṇṭa aracu cintu nākarikattil iruntataṟkāṉa taṭayaṅkaḷ illi eṉpar pocel (2002:6) “The socio - cultural form that the Indus polity took is not known”. eṉavē cintu nākarikam aṟṟi mocopotāmiya, ekiptu nākarikaṅkaḷip pōlum, piṟṟi (ki.pi.nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil irunta vaṭa amerikka mayā, teṉamerikka peru nāṭṭu iṅkā nākarikaṅkaḷip pōlum paṉmuka vaḷarcciyaṭintiruntapōtilum (mēṟkaṇṭa aṭiyāḷaṅkaḷi uṭiya) aracu etuvum cintu nākarikattil illi. pūcāri - aracarkaḷum Priest Kings cintu nākarikattil illi. piṉ eppaṭippaṭṭa amippu an nākarikattip pala nūṟu āṇṭukaḷ aṅku pēṇi naṭattivantatu eṉpati iṉṉum tolliyalāḷarkaḷ orumitta karuttuṭaṉ niṟuvavilli; ulakilēyē ittaṉmi vāynta nākarikam vēṟu etuvum eṅkum eṉṟum iruntatu “To my knowledge, there is, for example, no close parallel to it, in either the Archeological or Ethnographic record.” eṉiṉum, cintu nākarikatti niruvakittu vanta amippu (ēṉiya kīḻi nāṭukaḷip pōṉṟu) koṭuṅkōṉmi vāyntatākat tōṉṟavilli. ammakkaḷiṉ aṉittup piriviṉarumē ōraḷavu camattuvattuṭaṉ ceḻittu vāḻntaṉar eṉak karutalām (There is no reason for us to believe that it was despotic, in the oriental fashion p.247 [no basis] to rule out the notion that the entire Indus population was generally well-off, possibly relatively egalitarian (p.175) iviyē pocel karuttukaḷ.)

19. ulakil vēṟu paṇṭiya nākarikam etilum illāta oru taṉik kōṭpāṭṭik ṉinaṭiṭaṭipala koṇṭu uruvāṉa cintu nākarika vīḻccikku antak kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi ceyalpaṭṭa amippukaḷē cumār 600 āṇṭukaḷukku mēl akkōṭpāṭṭit toṭarntu pēṇi varamuṭiyāta nili ēṟpaṭṭatē (a flaw in internal structure)  kāraṇamākalām. evvaḷavutāṉ nallatāka iruntālum oru kōṭpāṭu nirantaramāka nilipeṟṟu, aḻivillāmal irukka muṭiyumā? mēlē viḷakkiyatum, neṭuṅkālam muraṇpāṭu conflict  iṉṟi nilittirunta cintuk kōṭpāṭum Indus ideology) oru kālattil muṭivukku vantu tāṉē ākavēṇṭum; (“too well adapted for its own good;” too much of a good thing) eṉavē cintu nākarikam muṭivaṭintataṟku mēlē iyal 1 iṉ iṟutiyil ālciṉ kuṟitta kāraṇaṅkaḷuṭaṉ ikkāraṇattiyum cērttuk koḷḷalām.

20. mikkēl vuṭ (2007: The story of India) nālāyiram muttirikaḷ mutaliyavi kiṭittiruppiṉum pōr, koli, koḷḷi, rattak kaḷari, ‘veṉṟavaṉ muṉ kitikaḷ’ pōṉṟa kāṭcikaḷ cittarikkap paṭātati viyakkiṟār. ki.mu. nāṉkām nūṟṟāṇṭil alek cāṇṭaruṭaṉ intiyāvukku vanta kirēkkarkaḷum‘intiyarkaḷ akkiramap pōri veṟuttaṉar; aṟaneṟi pōṟṟiṉar’ eṉa eḻutukiṉṟaṉar. vaṉ muṟiyi veṟutta piṟṟi intiya camayaṅkaḷ camaṇam, puttam (av viraṇṭukkum muntiyatāka ka. neṭuñceḻiyaṉ mutaliyavarkaḷ karutum ācīvakam) ākiyaṉavellām cintu nākarikak kālattilēyē karuk koṇṭavi eṉṟu pala aṟiñar karutuvatic camayam paṟṟiya piṉ varum iyalil kāṇka. ik karutukōḷ cariyeṉṟāl vaṉ muṟiyē aṭippaṭiyākak koṇṭa im mānta iṉattiṉ varalāṟṟil cintu nākarikam taṉippaṭṭa ciṟappuṭiyatu eṉpar vuṭ (But if anything like that were true, it would be unique in the violent history of humanity) 





vaṇṭi pommi
			
naṭaṉa mātiṉ paṭivam- veṇkalam 		tāḻip putippu
(mokeñ) 


iyal 5

cintuveḷi eṭi aḷavukaḷ
(tamiḻariṉ eṭi aḷavukaḷi ottavi)

tamiḻariṭiyē aṇmik kālam vari naṭimuṟiyil irunta “ciṟṟilakka” aṭippaṭiyil aminta eṇ muṟiyē cintu veḷi nākarikattilum naṭimuṟiyil iruntatu eṉṟu kaṇṭaṟintu veḷiyiṭṭa perumi poṟiyiyal aṟiñar ceṅkam vaḷiyāmpaṭṭu ku. vēṅkaṭācalam uṭiyatākum. (A study of the Weights and Measures of the Indus valley civilisation; 1983 tamiḻp palkalik kaḻakak karuttaraṅkuk kaṭṭuri) ituvari ulakil kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭuḷḷa eṭi aḷavu muṟikaḷilēyē toṉmiyāṉatu itutāṉ (Berriman: Historical Metrology; 1953) 

2. cintuveḷi eccaṅkaḷil ērāḷamāṉa akēṭ maṟṟum cakki mukkikkal eṭik kaṟkaḷum aṭaṅkum. makkēyiṉ aṟikki (1938) yiṉ 17vatu iyalil hemmi ivveṭikaḷip piṉvarumāṟu paṭṭiya liṭṭuḷḷār:

mēṟkaṇṭa piṉṉaṅkaḷukku iṭippaṭṭa cila maṭaṅkukaḷum tamiḻ nāṭṭil iruntaṉa. cintu veḷiyil kaṇṭeṭutta eṭikaḷellām ic ciṟṟilakka vāyppāṭṭu eṇmāṉa aṭippaṭiyil amintaṉavē eṉpati vēṅkaṭācalam teḷivāka viḷakkiyuḷḷār. eṭi aḷavukaḷ paṟṟi vētaṅkaḷ, arttacāstiram pōṉṟavaṟṟuḷ arikuṟiyākac collappaṭṭuḷḷaviyum cintuveḷi eṭikaḷip (ciṟṟilakka aṭippaṭiyila mintavi) piṉpaṟṟiyaviyē eṉpatiyum; intiyāvil palvēṟu pakutikaḷilum (1956 l meṭrik eṭic caṭṭam naṭimuṟikku varum vari) naṭimuṟiyilirunta eṭi aḷavukaḷ ellāmē cintuveḷi (tamiḻiya) eṭi aḷavukaḷip piṉpaṟṟi yaviyē eṉpatiyum avarniṟuviyuḷḷār. ki.mu.500kku muntiya tolkāppiya cūttiraṅkaḷilēyē eṭi, eṇmāṉa, aḷavīṭṭuc coṟkaḷ pala kuṟippiṭappaṭṭu avaṟṟukkāṉa puṇarcci mutaliya ilakkaṇa vitikaḷ kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷati vēṅkaṭācalam viḷakki tamiḻariṭiyē ikkōṭpāṭukaḷiṉ toṉmiyic cuṭṭukiṟār.

4. tamiḻarukkēyuriya 84cemī (33 aṅkula = iraṇṭēmukkāl aṭi) taccumuḷam cintuveḷik kālattiliruntu kaṭanta 5000 āṇṭukaḷākat toṭarntu vantuḷḷamiyi koṭu muṭi caṇmukam kuṟittuḷḷār. (Journal of Tamil Studies, cūṉ 1980) cintu veḷit
terukkaḷil pala 33’, 11’ aṭi eṉṟa aḷavil iruntatik kaṇṭōm. 11 eṉpatu 23/4 aṭiyiṉ nāṉku maṭaṅku lōtalil kaṇṭeṭukkappaṭṭa caṅku ōṭṭil ceyta oru ciṟiya aṭikkōlil 1.32 aṅkulam 5 pakutikaḷāka (ovvoṉṟum 0.264 aṅkulam) pirikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. taccumuḻam 33 aṅkulatti 25 ākap pirittāl ovvoru pirivum 1.32 aṅkulam varum eṉpati nōkkuka. tañcip peruvuṭiyār kōvilum tiruvaṇṇāmaliyār kōyilum iraṇṭē mukkāl aṭi taccumuḻa aḷaviṉ paṭiyē kaṭṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa.

iyal 6

camayam

1. camayam (matam) eṉpatu teyvaṅkaḷik kuṟittu māntar nampuvatik (maṟupiṟavi, maṟuulaku, toṉmaṅkaḷ uṭpaṭa) kuṟippa tākum. annampikkikaḷ meyppikkavō, poyppikkavō muṭiyātavi (can be neither affirmed nor falsified) kaṭavuḷ, āṉmikam cārnta inta nampikkiyip pōṉṟaviyē araciyal, poruḷātāram paṟṟi avvappoḻutu nilavi varum, palvēṟu “icaṅkaḷum” (isms) avi paṟṟiya nampikkikaḷum ākum.

2. cintu nākarikac ciṉṉaṅkaḷ 1921 liruntu akaḻvāyvil veḷippaṭat toṭaṅkiṉa. āyiṉum ataṟkumuṉṉarē (ēṉ 19-m nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṭiyiliruntē) kālṭuvel, maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ uḷḷiṭṭa aṟiñar palar āriyar ki.mu.1500 ai oṭṭi intiyāvukkuḷ nuḻiyumuṉṉar ciṟanta tamiḻiya / tirāviṭa nākarikam intiyā muḻuvatum paraviyiruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟum piṟkāla intiyar vāḻviyal, camayam uṭpaṭa, tamiḻiya nākarikatti aṭippaṭiyākak koṇṭatē eṉṟa karutti terivittiruntatu paṟṟiya muḻuvivaraṅkaḷ piṟa iyal kaḷilum uḷḷaṉa.

3. cintu nākarika camayattip paṟṟi 2001 il ār.ci.taṇṭā ‘hārappāvil tōṉṟiya intu camayam’ Harappan origin of Hindiusm  mukkiyamāṉa nūlākum intiyāvil iṉṟum vaḻaṅkivarum intu camayattip pōṉṟa camayamē cintu nākarikattilum vaḻaṅkiyatu eṉṟa carjāṉ mārṣalkarutti iṉṟu aṉittu aṟiñarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. (“Their religiion is so characteristically Indian, as hardly to be distinguished from the still living Hinduism”) 

4. cintu nākarikattil cumēriyā, ekiptu nākarikaṅkaḷil iruntatu pōṉṟa periya kaṭṭumāṉak kōyilkaḷ illi eṉiṉum caṅkakālat tamiḻakattil iruntatupōla marattaṭi vaḻipāṭu pōṉṟavi paravalāka iruntirukkum. vīṭukaḷilum kaṭavuḷ uruvaṅkaḷ, takaṭukaḷ, tāyattukkaḷ pōṉṟavi vaḻipāṭṭukkup payaṉpaṭṭirukkalām. muttirikaḷil uḷḷa uruvaṅkaḷiliruntu aracamaram pōṉṟa maravaḻipāṭu erutu, yāṉi
pōṉṟa vilaṅkukaḷ vaḻipāṭu, peṇ teyva vaḻipāṭu pōṉṟavi iruntatu uṇarappaṭukiṟatu. ‘pacupati’ muttiri (mokañcotarō: 420) yōki uruvil aminta civaṉik kuṟippatu eṉpatu mārṣal karuttu. māṟāka atu peṇteyvam eṉṟu karutupavarkaḷ ec.pi.calli vāṉ (1964), ē.hiltepeytal (1978), cupaṅkaṇā ātrē (1987) ākiyōr āvar; ikkaruttu potuvāka ēṟkappaṭavilli. āṭavallāṉ paṭimattiṟku muṉṉōṭiyeṉak karutattakka cili hārappāvil kiṭitti mēlē kaṇṭōm. liṅka vaṭivaṅkaḷ palavum kiṭit tuḷḷaṉa. muttirikaḷil oṉṟu camaṇa riṣapa tēvarik kuṟippa tākalām - (ār.pi.cantā: māṭarṉ riviyū , ākasṭu 1932). cakkaramum ‘cuvattikamum’ ñāyiṟṟik kuṟippatākaliṉ ñāyiṟu vaḻipāṭum irun tirukkum eṉpar cilar. kiṭittuḷḷa ciṟu ciṟu patumikaḷil cila nērttik kaṭaṉākak kōyilkaḷukku vaḻaṅkappaṭṭaviyeṉṟum karutu kiṉṟaṉar. teppakkuḷam, nīrāṭal mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ mutaṉmiyi liruntum piṟkāla intucamaya muṉṉōṭi in nākarikattil kāṇak kiṭakkiṟatu.

5. tirāviṭariṉ tāyt teyvam koṟṟavi, paḻiyōḷ, kāṉamar celvi, kāṭu kiḻāḷ, kaṭalkeḻucelvi eṉap pala peyarkaḷāl aḻikkappaṭṭāḷ. avaḷē cēyōṉākiya murukaṉiṉ tāy (perum pāṇāṟṟup paṭi : 457) hārappā nākarika tāyt teyvamum koṟ ṟaviyiṉ vaṭivē eṉpatu pi.el. cāmi karuttu. lōtalil vaḻipaṭṭatāka aṟiyappaṭum ‘vaṉavāci koṭṭarimātā’ vaṭivam kāṉamar celviyē; elliyammaṉ (ellammaṉ) maṟu uruvamē rēṇukā eṉpatum avar karuttu. ivvaṉittup peṇ teyvaṅkaḷiṉ iṇippiṉ kāraṇa mākavē piṟkālattil lajja kuri pōṉṟa peṇ teyvaṅkaḷ vaṭanāṭṭil uruvāyiṉar eṉkiṟār avar (Journal of Tamil Studies December 1992.)  ‘mo. 430’ muttiri pōṉṟavaṟṟil kāṇappaṭum ēḻukaṉṉiyar vaṭivaṅkaḷ kārttikip peṇkaḷik kuṟikkalām eṉpar parpōlā (South Asian Archeology, 1987)  vaṭa intiyar tāyt teyvatti Hoi (She happened : āy?) eṉṟa peyaril vaḻipaṭukiṉṟaṉar eṉpar taṇṭā (pak. 190)

6. intiyāvil camaya vaḷarcciyil kuṟippiṭattakkatu oṉṟu uṇṭu. uyirkaḷ tam viṉip payaṉukkēṟpa mīṇṭum mīṇṭum piṟavi yeṭukkum eṉpatu putta matattiṉ mukkiya koḷki. puttamatam tōṉṟiya kālattil tōṉṟiya camaṇamum ācīvakamum ak koḷki yuṭiyaviyē. piṟavit taḷiyiliruntu viṭupaṭuvatu camayattiṉ kuṟikkōḷ eṉpatu ammataṅkaḷiṉ koḷki. immūṉṟu camayaṅ kaḷum, cāṅkiya - yōkā kōṭpāṭukaḷum maṭṭumaṉṟi, vētāntak koḷkiyum āriyarallātavaruṭiyavi eṉpatu vallunar karuttu:-

ālparṭ cuviṭcar: ‘Vedanta is Brahminized Sankhya’ 
heyṉric jimmar‘Sankhya - Yoga, Jainism and early Buddhism
represent the thinking of non - Aryan people of India’. 

inta maṟupiṟavik koḷkiyi car villiyam jōṉs pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār. tattamatu kaṭavuḷ coṉṉatākak kūṟappaṭum camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷi mīṟum maṉitar mīḷā narakattil eṉṟeṉṟum muṭiviṉṟic cittira vatikkuṭpaṭuvar eṉṟa kurūramāṉa kōṭpāṭṭik koṇṭavi (kiṟittuvam, iculām pōṉṟa) cemittiya mataṅkaḷ; akkōṭpāṭṭi viṭa intiya camayaṅkaḷiṉ maṟu piṟavik koḷki evvaḷavō aṟivu pūrvamāṉatu; kaṭavuḷ uṇarccikkukantatu; tīmiyiṉpāṟ cellātu maṉitarik taṭukkavallatu eṉa jōṉs kūṟiyuḷḷār.

7. jē.el. pirākkiṅṭaṉ itupaṟṟik kūṟuvatu ( The Sacred Thread); 

viṉip payaṉi nampuvatu eṉpatu maṉitaṉ cuyamuyaṟciyik kiviṭṭu ‘naṭappatu naṭakkaṭṭum’ eṉa vāḷā irukkac ceyyum nili (fatalism)  eṉap potuvākak karutuvatu aṟiyāmiyākum. ‘muntic ceyalkaḷāl iṉṟiya nili eṟpaṭṭuḷḷatu. iṉṟu nām ceyyum ceyalkaḷ varuṅkālatti nirṇayikkum’ eṉṟu collum poḻutu ‘maṉitaṉiṉ iṉṟiya nili taṉ ceyalāl ēṟpaṭṭatu; varuṅ kālattiyum taṉ ceyalāl nirṇayikkalām” eṉṟu aṟivuṟuttuva tākavē koḷḷa vēṇṭum.

paḻiya upaniṭataṅkaḷil maṟupiṟavik koḷki putumiyāṉa tākavum cilarukku maṭṭum terinta maṟi poruḷākavum kuṟippiṭappaṭṭu iruntatu. iruntum piṉṉar akkoḷki putta camaṇa camayaṅkaḷ vaḻiyāka virintu aṉivarālum piṉpaṟṟap paṭṭatu evvāṟu? enta makkaḷ, camūkattiṉariṭam iruntu puttar tōṉṟiṉārō avarka ḷiṭiyē ak koḷki paravalākat toṉṟu toṭṭu vaḻaṅki vanta koḷkiyāka atu iruntirukkavēṇṭum. eṉavētāṉ atu virivil aṉittu makkaḷiṉ kōṭpāṭāka ēṟṟuk koḷḷappaṭṭatu

8. “vaṭamēṟkiliruntu nuḻinta āriyarkaḷāl makatam mutaliya kīḻttici nāṭukaḷilirunta āriyarallāta mēṉmakkaḷ muḻumiyāka iṭam peyarntilar. attakiya mēṉmakkaḷ kuṭumpaṅkaḷ pala toṭarntu iruntaṉa. āriya āḷum kuṭumpaṅkaḷ valuviḻantu vīḻat toṭaṅkiya piṉ muntiya uḷḷūr āḷum kuṭumpaṅkaḷ mīṇṭum valuppeṟṟaṉa. eṭuttukkāṭṭāka cantirakupta muriyaṉ ittakiya kuṭumpattic cārntavaṉ. puttarum avvāṟē” eṉṟu heyṉric cimmar (Heinrich Zimmer: The Philosophies of India; 1951) ceppiyuḷḷatiyum karutuka.

9. intu camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷil aṭippaṭiyākavum ciṟantaṉa vākavum uḷḷaviyellām vaṭanāṭṭil paravalāka 3000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉ vāḻnta tamiḻ mēṉmakkaḷiṭamiruntu peṟappaṭṭavi eṉpati yum avaṟṟi āriyaruṭiyaṉavākak koḷvatu aṟiyāmi eṉpatiyum maṟimali aṭikaḷ tamiḻar nākarikam allatu vēḷāḷar yār? (1923); māṇikkavācakar varalāṟum kālamum (1930); tamiḻar matam (1941) pōṉṟa nūlkaḷil niṟuviyuḷḷār. kā.cuppiramaṇiya piḷḷitamiḻarcamayam (1940) tēvanēyap pāvāṇartamiḻar matam (1972) ākiyōrukkum aṭikaḷ karuttu uṭaṉpāṭēyākum.

10.rik vēta kālattiliruntē (ki.mu.1000) “kuṭi, koli malinta āriya vēḷviyiṉ tīviṉip peṟṟiyuñ ciṟu teyva vaṇakkac ciṟumiyun tēṟṟi aṉpum aruḷum perukac ceyta tamiḻc cāṉṟōrum uḷar. avar aruḷic ceytaṉavē muttī vēḷviyum uruttira vaḻipāṭuñ ciṟanteṭuttuk kūṟum irukku, ecur, cāma, atarva vētap patikaṅkaḷ cila palavum; uṇmi vaḻā upaniṭataṅ kaḷum; cāṅkiya, yōka, vicēṭika, niyāyika, vētānta cūttiraṅ kaḷum; maṟcam, vāyu mutalāṉa cila purāṇaṅkaḷum; puṭkaram, mirukēn tiram mutalāṉa cila civākamaṅkaḷum piṟavumām’ eṉpatu aṭikaḷ 1930 il eḻutiya muṭivu. im muṭivukkāṉa eṭuttukkāṭṭukaḷāka maṟi mali aṭikaḷ tantuḷḷa palavaṟṟuḷ mutaṉmiyāṉa cila varumāṟu:-

‘irukku vētattiṟ ‘paratar’ mutaliya peyarkaḷāl vaḻaṅkap paṭṭa paṇṭit tamiḻ naṉmakkaḷē (“paratar’ eṉṉum peyariliruntē ‘pāratam” uruvāṉatu). ñāyiṟu, tiṅkaḷ, tī eṉṉum muttī vaṭivil vittu muḻumutaṟ kaṭavuḷāṉa civapirāṉi vaḻipaṭum nuṭpa muṟiyik kaṇṭōrāvar’ (1930)

‘vicuvāmittirar eṉṉum tamiḻaraca muṉivarāl ceytu cērkkappaṭṭa irukku vēta mūṉṟām maṇṭilattil uḷḷa kāyattiri mantiramāṉatu ñāyiṟṟu maṇṭilattiṉ kaṇ muḷittu viḷaṅkum parkkaṉ eṉṉum peyaruṭiya civapirāṉ mēṟṟāy viḷaṅkukiṟatu’ (1923)

“kollā aṟatti mutaṉmutaṟ kaṇṭaṟintu ataṟ kēṟpat tamatu immi vāḻkkiyi naṭattiṉavarkaḷ tamiḻaril mēṉmakkaḷāy irunta oru perum pakutiyārē ... maṟṟi makkaṭ piriviṉariluḷ enta mēlōrum ivvarumaruntaṉṉa uṇmiyiṉik kaṇṭaṟintilar ... vaṭakkilirunta camaṇa, cākkiyar, cāṅkiyar, yōkar mutalāyiṉāruṅ kollā vaṟattiṉi viṭāppiṭiyāyk koṇṭu oḻukiṉaralla rō veṉil; camaṇa cākkiyar mutalāṉa avarellām āriyar varutaṟku muṉṉamē imayamali variyiṟ paravi nākarikattiṟ ciṟantu uyir vāḻnta vaṭanāṭṭuta tamiḻ mēṉmakkaḷiṉ marapil vantavarē’ (1940)

11. tamatu cāti vēṟṟumiyum pōliccivarum (1923) nūlil maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ virivāka viḷakkum piṉvarum ceytikaḷ ivviyal, aṭutta iyal iraṇṭiṉ poruḷkaḷukkumē toṭarpuṭi yaṉavākum. eṉiṉum vacati karuti ivviyalilēyē tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. (nuṇmāṇuḻipulamikka akkaruttukaḷ vēta / camaskiruta moḻi, paṇpāṭṭu āyvāḷarkaḷ (innāṭṭavarum cari, mēṉāṭṭavarum cari) yārālum ivvaḷavu teḷivāka terivikkappaṭavilli):

nālvaki makkaṭpiriviṉariṉ peyari avvavar toḻil veṟupāṭupaṟṟi eṭuttōtum patikam ip puruṭacūtta mantirattit tavira vēṟētum irukku vētattil eṅkuṅ kāṇappaṭāmi yāṉum, oṉpatāñ ceyyuḷil irukku, ecur cāmam eṉṉum mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷiṉ peyari eṭuttuc collum ip puruṭa cūttam am mūṉṟuvētaṅ kaḷun tōṉṟiya piṉṉaṉṟi muṉṉiruntatu ākāmi yāṉum, nālvēṟu makkaṭ pākupāṭum ataṉi yeṭuttuk kūṟum puruṭacūttamumirukku, ecur ,cāmam eṉṉum mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷum iyaṟṟappaṭṭakālattil iruntaṉa alla eṉpatum uṇarntu koḷḷal vēṇṭum.

iṉi, māpāratap pōril muṉintu niṉṟa kurukula pāñcāla aracar kaḷiyum, avar vaḻiyil vanta jaṉakaṉ ajātacatturu, jaṉamējaya, parikṣit, ivaṟku āciriyaṉākiya vicampāyaṉaṉ mutaliyōri yum eṭuttōtumkiruṣṇa cuklayajur vētamum ataṉōṭu oru kālattākiya cāma vētamum am māpāratappōr nikaḻntataṟkuppiṉ ākkap paṭṭaṉavātal teḷiyappaṭum. ivviraṇṭu vētaṅkaḷ iyaṟṟappaṭṭa kālattiyaṭuttē, av vētaṅkaḷukku viḷakka urikaḷāy eḻunta ‘pirāmaṇaṅ’kaḷum avaṟṟiyaṭutta vēḷvi vēṭṭali maṟuttu muḻu mutaṟkaṭavuḷiṉ meym miyip pulappaṭuttavum paṉṉiraṇṭu paḻiya upaniṭataṅkaḷum ākkappaṭṭaṉa. ivikaḷuṇṭāṉa kālattiluṅ kūṭap, piṟappaḷavil cātivēṟupāṭu collappaṭa villi. cukla yacur vētattiṉ muppatām iyaliṟ pala vēṟu toḻilkaḷic ceyyum palvēṟu makkaṭkūṭṭattiṉar peyarkaḷ maṭṭumē eṭutturikkappaṭ ṭirukkiṉṟaṉa; iṅṅaṉamē tittirīya pirāmaṇattiluñ collap paṭṭiruk kiṉṟaṉa; āṉāl, avaṟṟuṭ cātivēṟupāṭukaḷum, avaṟṟiṉ uyarpu iḻipukaḷum pēcappaṭa villi. ataṟku oru cāṉṟu eṭuttuk kāṭṭutum: muṉṉoru kāl caracuvati yāṟṟaṅkariyilē iruṭikaḷ oru vēḷvi vēṭkalāyiṉar. appōtu ‘illūṣi’ eṉṉum ōr aṭimip peṇṇiṉ putalvarāṉa ‘kavaṣā’ eṉpavar av vēḷvikku vantiruntaṉar. avarkaḷ avari ōr aṭimiyiṉ piḷḷi eṉpatu paṟṟi ikaḻntu, av vēḷvikkaḷattiṉiṉṟun turatta, avar ceṉṟa iṭattiṟkē avyāṟṟu nīr cellumāṟu kaṭavuḷ aruḷceytaṉar; ataṉiyuṇarnta iruṭikaḷ avariyikaḻnta tamatu aṟiyāmikku varuntip piṉṉar avari oru ciṟanta iruṭiyāka ēṟṟu vaṇaṅkiṉar; eṉṟu aitrēyapirāmaṇam (2, 19) pukalukiṉṟatu. ik ‘kavaṣā’ eṉpavarē irukku vētattiṉ pattām maṇṭilattiṟ pala patikaṅkaḷi iyaṟṟi yirukkiṉṟaṉar. ivvāṟṟāṟpirāmaṇaṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭa kālattilum cāti vēṟṟumi nilipeṟavilli yeṉpatu pulaṉākiṉṟataṉṟō?

iṉṉum, pala vēḷvikaḷic ceytu muṭittavaṉum, av vēḷvikaḷilmuṉivararkkum piṟarkkum ērāḷamāṉa naṉkoṭi vaḻaṅkiṉavaṉum, vicuvā mittira muṉivarip pātukāttavaṉum, irukku vēta ēḻām maṇṭilattiṉ 18 ām patikattiṟ pārāṭṭap paṭṭavaṉu māṉa ‘pijavaṉaṉ’ eṉpāṉ oru cūttiraṉē eṉṟu māpāratam cāntiparvam 2304 ām ceyyuḷiliruntu nuvalkiṉṟatu.

iṉṉum,irukkuvētap patikaṅkaḷi ākkiya muṉivarar palarum araca vakuppiṉiyum vaṇika vakuppiṉiyuñ cērntōr āvareṉṟu maṟcapurāṇattiṉ 132ām iyal vakutturikkiṉṟatu. irukku vēta ttiṉ mūṉṟām maṇṭilatti ākkiya vicuvāmittirarum, maṟṟi maṇṭilaṅkaḷiṟ patikaṅkaḷ palavaṟṟi ākkiya vivacuva tamaṉu, iṭaṉ, purūravar mutalāyiṉārum araca vakuppiṉarē āvar; palantar, vantiyar, caṅkīrtti mutaliyōr viciya vakuppiṉa rāvar.irukku vēta ttiṉ, mutal maṇṭilattuḷḷa 100 ām patikatti iyaṟṟiya rijrācuvar, amparīṣar, cakatēvar, payamā nar, curātar mutalāṉa iruṭikaḷ aivarum viruṣākir aracaṉ putalvarāvar eṉṟu irukku vētattiṉ anuk kiramaṇikiyē pukalkiṉṟatu. iṅṅaṉamē, av virukkuvētattiṉ 6 āvatu maṇṭilattiluḷḷa 15 ām patikattic ceyta vītahavyarum, 10 āvatu maṇṭilattu 9 ām patikatti ākkiya amparīṭar makaṉāṉa cintut vīparum, ataṉ 75 ām patikatti iyaṟṟiya piriya mētar makaṉāṉa cintukṣittarum, ataṉ 179 ām patikattip pāṭiya ucīnarariṉ makaṉ cipiyum, kācimaṉṉaṉ tivōtācaṉ makaṉ piratarttaṉarum, ataṉ 98 ām patikattip pukaṉṟa cantanuviṉ makaṉ tēvāpiyum ellām araca iṉattic cērn tōrēyāvar. ivvāṟē iṉṉum palar uḷar.

mēṟcolliya irukkuvētap patikaṅkaḷi ākkiya cattiriya viciyar eṉpār tamiḻa vēḷāḷa vakuppiṉarē yāvar. yāṅṅaṉa meṉiṟ kūṟutum: mēṟkāṭṭiya rijrācuvar mutalāṉa araca muṉivar aivarum cērntiyaṟṟiya patikattiṉ 12 ām ceyyuḷil ‘cōmaṉ’ eṉṉuṅ kaṭavuḷ aintu iṉattārik kāppavarākac collappaṭu kiṉṟaṉar. ic ‘cōmaṉ’ eṉṉuñ col ‘ca umā’ eṉṉum iru moḻip puṇarcciyāṟ ṟōṉṟi ‘umiyōṭu kūṭiṉavar’ eṉṟu poruḷpaṭum eṉa atarvaciras upaniṭata uriyilē viḷakkappaṭṭiruttaliṉ, ac col civapirāṉ mēṟṟātalum, ‘cōmanātam’ eṉṉum vaṭanāṭṭuc civa pirāṉ tirukkōyil ap peyarpeṟṟiruttaliṉ aḵtu ataṟkup piṉṉum oru cāṉṟātalum naṉku viḷaṅkum. iṉic civapirāṉāṟ kākkappaṭum aivaki iṉattiṉar āvār turuvācar kaḷum yatukkaḷum aṇukkaḷum turukiyarkaḷum pūrukkaḷum eṉa aivar avareṉṟu kiripit eṉṉum āciriyar naṉkeṭuttuk kāṭṭiyirukkiṉṟār. (rik vēta āṅkila vaṭivam, I pak. 10) iv vivaki iṉattiṉarum tamiḻmakkaṭ piriviṉarē yāva reṉṟu rākociṉ eṉṉum varalāṟṟu nūlāciriyar tām āḻntārāynteḻutiya vēta intiyā (pak. 323-9) eṉṉum nūlil virittu viḷakkiyirukkiṉṟār. 2 vaṭakkiruntu vantu intiya nāṭṭuḷ nuḻinta āriya makkaḷi etirtta pattu aracar kaḷukkut talivarāy niṉṟōr pūrukkaḷum, avarkkut tuṇivarāy añcāneñciṉarāy nilaviya paratarkaḷum āvar. it tamiḻa aracar patiṉmaruṭ civar (civar) viṣāniyar (viṣṇavar) eṉpār iruvarum cērttuc collap paṭukiṉṟaṉar (rikvētam 7, 18). ākavē, attuṇip paḻiya kālattilēyē tamiḻaruṭ civa pirāṉi vaṇaṅkuñ civarum, viṣṇuvi vaṇaṅkum viṣṇavarum valiyarāyiruntu āriyari etirttu niṉṟami pulaṉākum. paḻiya tamiḻ makkaḷuṭ pōr valimiyil mikac ciṟantuniṉṟa paratar tam kuṭiyiṟ ṟōṉṟiya aracarkaḷē iv intiya nāṭu muḻumiyum āṇṭuvantaṉar; atupaṟṟiyē, avarkaḷiṉ ceṅkōl niḻalil vantu vikiya āriya makkaḷ iv intiya nāṭṭip “pārata varṣam’ eṉṟu aḻippārāyiṉar. kumari nāṭu kaṭalkoṇṭapiṉ aṅkirunta tamiḻarkaḷē vaṭanāṭu variyiṟ ceṉṟu āṅkāṅkuk kuṭiyēṟip pattuvaki araciyali nāṭṭi ṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷuḷ vaṭamaturiyil aracāṇṭa yatu kulattavarkaḷē piṉṉark kaṇṇaṉit tali vaṉāyk koṇṭu tuvārakiyil vantu kuṭiyēṟiṉar. av yatu kulattavaril oru pakutiyārē peyarttun tamiḻnāṭṭiṟ pōntu erumiyūriṟ kuṭiyēṟiṉar(R.C.Dutt:  ai pak. 19) erumiyūriṟ kuṭiyēṟiya yatukulattavarākiya vēḷirkkut talivaṉē puṟanāṉūṟṟiṟ kūṟappaṭum iruṅkō vēḷ eṉṉum maṉṉaṉ āvāṉ. ivvāṟu ivar pōṟ ṟamiḻ nāṭṭiṉum piṟanāṭukaḷiṉum an nāḷiṟ paraviyirunta vēḷāḷa maṉṉarkaḷ varalāṟukaḷi urikkap pukiṉ itu mika viriyum. it tamiḻa vēḷāḷa aracarkaḷiyē paḻiya vaṭamoḻivētaṅkaḷum upaniṭataṅkaḷum ‘rājanyar’, ‘cattiriyar’ eṉṉum peyarkaḷāṟ kuṟippavāyiṉa eṉpataṟku, maṉu tirāviṭarkaḷic ‘cattiriyar’ eṉṟu kūṟutalē (10, 43, 44) cāṉṟām. tamiḻa vēḷāḷaril uḻavuttoḻil ceytārum, uḻavut toḻilāṟ peṟṟa paṇṭaṅkaḷik koṇṭu viṟṟārumē vaṭanūlkaḷil ‘viciyar’ eṉṟu nuvalappaṭuvārā yiṉar. ivvāṟu kāṭṭappaṭṭa cattiriyarum viciyarumākiya tamiḻa vēḷāḷarē toṉṟu toṭṭuc civapirāṉi vaṇaṅki varupavarāy iruttaliṉ, am makkaṭpiriviṉar aivariyuṅ kāppavar ‘cōmaṉ’ eṉṉum civapirāṉāka irukku vētam pukaluvatāyiṟṟeṉka. eṉavē, irukku vētattiṟ palappala patikaṅkaḷ iyaṟṟiya cattiriya viciyarkaḷ aṉivarum tamiḻa vēḷāḷarkaḷē yātalit teṟṟeṉa uṇarntukoḷka.

ivvāṟu cattiriya viciyarākiya vēḷāḷarkaḷum ivar tamakkuk kuṟṟēvaṟṟoḻil purivārāṉa cūttirarum, maṟṟi āriyarum kalantu vētaṅkaḷiyum pirāmaṇaṅkaḷiyum paṉṉiraṇṭu upaniṭataṅkaḷi yum ākkivitta kālattiṟ, piṟappaḷavil tamakkuḷ uyarvu tāḻvu kāṇātu, uyirvāḻkkikku iṉṟi yamiyāta nālvēṟu toḻilkaḷiyam ceytu koṇṭu uṇṇal kalattalkaḷil ētoru vēṟupāṭu miṉṟi orumittu vāḻntaṉarākaliṉ, ak kālattiṟ piṟappaḷavil uyarvu tāḻvu koḷḷum cāti vēṟṟumi ciṟitumilliyeṉṟu teḷika. itaṉā laṉṟōvāyupurāṇamum (8, 190-3) ‘tīyavaṉukku, maṉattūymi iḻantavaṉukku vētaṅkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ caṭaṅkukaḷum vēḷvikaḷum paṭṭiṉi kiṭattalum teṉ pulattār kaṭaṉiṟuttal mutalāṉa viṉikaḷum payaṉit tarukiṉṟila; akattē iḻinta iyaṟkiyuṭiyavaṉāyiruppōṉ puṟattē ettuṇi muyaṟciyōṭu caṭaṅkukaḷic ceytāṉāyiṉum, avi ciṟitum payaṉpaṭā. oruvaṉ aḻukku neñcat tōṭu taṉakkuḷḷa poruḷ muḻutuṅ kāṭuttāṉēṉum, ataṉāl naṉmi eytamāṭṭāṉ; ātalāl nalla taṉmiyē uyarccikku ētuvākum,” eṉṟu kūṟuvatāyiṟṟu.

iṉi, mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷukkum piṟpaṭṭatākiya puruṭa cūttat tilum, ataṟkum piṟpaṭṭatākiya piruka tāraṇiyaka upaniṭatat tilum (1, 4, 15) nālvakic cātippeyar kāṇappaṭiṉum, muṉṉiyatil an nāṟ cātiyiṉarum oru muḻumutaṟkaṭavuḷiṉ piḷḷikaḷātalum, piṉṉiyatil annāṟcātiyiṉariṭattum iṟivaṉ niṟintu viḷaṅkutalum naṉku vaṟpuṟuttu urikkap paṭutaliṉ, in nūlkaḷ tōṉṟiya kālattil nālvakic cātikaḷ iruntami peṟappaṭiṉum, avaruḷ uyarvu tāḻvukaḷ iruntami pulaṉāka villi.

pirāmaṇaṅkaḷ tōṉṟuvataṟku muṟpaṭṭa vētakālat til pūṇūl aṇiyum vaḻakkaṅ kāṇappaṭāmiyāl, atupaṟṟi yuṇṭāṉa cāti vēṟṟumiyum aññāṉṟu iruntatilli. catapatapirāmaṇat tilum (2, 4, 2), kuṣītaki upaniṭatattilumē (2, 7) pūṇūl aṇiyuṅ kuṟippu mutaṉmutaṟ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. ivviraṇ ṭiluṅkūṭap pakalavaṉ kīḻpāl eḻumpōtu avaṉi vaṇaṅkuṅ kālippoḻutilum, vēḷvi vēṭkum poḻutilumē māntar pūṇūl aṇintu maṟṟik kālaṅkaḷil ataṉik kaḻaṟṟiviṭum vaḻakkam naṉku terintōtap paṭṭamiyāl, in nūlkaḷ uṇṭāṉa kālattilum cātivēṟṟumi yiṉ poruṭṭup pūṇūl aṇiyappaṭavilliyeṉpatum, iṟivaṉi vaḻipaṭun toḻiliṟ pukuntiruppōr attoḻiliṟ pukātār tammi aṇukāmal vilakippōtaṟku or aṭiyāḷa mākavē ataṉi attoḻil iyaṟṟum nēraṅkaḷil maṭṭum aṇintiruntaṉareṉpatum iṉitu viḷaṅkum.



āriyar maṭṭumaṉṟi āriyarallāta piṟarum (tirāviṭarum) rikvēta kālattilēyē pirāmaṇarāka, riṣikaḷāka ēṟkappaṭṭaṉar eṉpatu tāmōtara tarmānanta kōcampi (1950-51 On the Origin of the Brahmin gotras Jounal of the Bombay Branch of the Royal Asiatic Society  26.21-80l) uṭiya muṭivum ākum. attaki tirāviṭa riṣikaḷ tāṅkaḷ enta āriya aracaṉiṭam cērntukoṇṭaṉarō avaṉ kōttirattiyē tamatu kōttiramākak koṇṭaṉar (piṟkālattil itit talikīḻāka māṟṟi kuruvāṉa pirāmaṇaṉiṉ kōttiramē aracaṉukkum eṉṟu puṉintu koṇṭaṉar.) uṇminili ituvākiyāltāṉ intiyāvil palapakutikaḷilum uḷḷa pirāmaṇarum uṭalamippu, tōṟṟam ākiya uṭaliyaṟ kūṟukaḷil perumaḷavu māṟupaṭṭu uḷḷatōṭu, oru mānila pirāmaṇar iṉṉoru mānila pirāmaṇaruṭaṉ maṇa uṟavu koḷḷāta nilimiyum, oru mānilattiṟkuḷḷum uḷḷa palavaki pirāmaṇac cātiyiṉar tattamakkuḷ maṇa uṟavu koḷḷāta nilimiyum uḷḷateṉpar kōcampi.

“My thesis is that, specifically as regards some important Brahmins, the gotra system is adopted by small groups of pre-Ksatriya and pre - Aryan people from Aryan invaders; as these groups take the functions of priesthood, they are most logically assigned to the patriarchal clan-group of those for whom they officiate. They consequently acquire the same gotra; only afterwards does the rule become its opposite, when the Vedic Kshatrias have died out by the rise of settlements and the emergence of other warriors of obscure origin who fight their way to the top. At that stage, it becomes quite possible to assign to these newcomers, the same gotra as that of the priests, who have mintined a continuity of tradition and acquired a monopoly of scripture by long and arduous study. I do not mean to imply that all gotras, or even all Brahmin gotras originated in this way.

xxx. gotra lists could not be closed, and newcomers were obviously being recruited into the ranks of the priesthood. The Nagara Brahmins of Gujarat are supposed to be medieval immigrants. If the institution of marriage were so stricty bounded by caste and gotra rules, it would be difficult to explin the strong racial heterogeneity of Brahmins in India, as well as the existence of endogamous regional units within them (amounting to sub - castes) which have no basis in scripture)
- D.D. Kosambi, 1950 - 51 

12. aṭikaḷāriṉ mēṟcoṉṉa karuttukkaḷiṉ karuvip piṉvaru māṟu kā. appātturiyār 1956 il “1800 āṇṭukaṭku muṟpaṭṭa tamiḻakam ” tamiḻākkattiṉ aṭik kuṟippil) curuṅkak kuṟittār:

“tamiḻar paṇṭiyac camaya vāḻvu iṉṟiya pala camayaṅkaḷiṉ uruvākāmūlak karu mutalit taṉṉiṭa mākak koṇṭa vāḻvē yākum. atil ellāc camayamūlamum kāṇalām... putta, camaṇa, civa, viṇavak karuttukkaḷ uruvākumuṉ, ac camayap peyarkaḷ vēṟu vēṟākat tamiḻar neṟiyiṉiṉṟu piriyu muṉ, āriyar iṭiniṉṟu tamiḻariṉ oruneṟiyip palavāṟu pirikkumuṉ avvellā neṟi kaḷukkum vaḻikāṭṭiya mika muṟpaṭṭa kālat tamiḻ aṟivar marapucārnta aṟiñaruḷ oruvarē tiruvaḷḷuvar eṉpatu uyttuṇarattakka uṇmiyākum.”

13. maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ karuttukkaḷip pōṉṟē vēṟupala aṟiñar terivittuḷḷa icinta karuttukkaḷ varumāṟu:-

(i) “namakkuk kiṭikkum takavalkaḷellām orupakkattiliruntu kiṭikkum (pirāmaṇac cārpāṉa) takavalkaḷē. muḻut takavalkaḷum kiṭṭiyiruntāl intiyar camayam tirāviṭar camayamē (iṅkuvanta āriyarkaḷ cintaṉikkēṟpac ceyyappaṭṭa cila māṟṟaṅkaḷukkuṭ paṭṭu) eṉpati uṇarntu iruppōm.

“pirāmaṇac camutāyattōṭu muḻumiyāka oppiṭakkūṭiya amippu ulakil vēṟu enta nāṭṭilum illi. kattōlikkap pātirikaḷip pōlavō, mukamatiya mullākkaḷip pōlavō oru kuṟippiṭṭa kōṭpāṭṭik kiḷippiḷḷi pōla kālā kālamum tūkkippiṭittu valiyuṟutti varuvatalla pirāmaṇar taṉmi. intiyā vil uruvākum allatu nuḻiyum entac camayattiyum kōṭ pāṭṭiyum kālattukkut takka māṟṟaṅkaḷuṭaṉ taṅkaḷ talimiyil ceyalpaṭuttak karutum cintaṉiyāḷa mēṭṭukkuṭip parampari tāṅkaḷtām eṉpavar avarkaḷ”.

“pirāmaṇaruṭiya nōkkam ‘kuṟippāka inta camayattittāṉ parappa vēṇṭum’ eṉpatāka eṉṟum iruntatilli. intiyāvil paravum entac camayamāyiṉum cari ataṉi vaḻinaṭattik kiyāḷupavarkaḷ tāṅkaḷāka irukkavēṇṭum eṉpatē avarkaḷ kuṟikkōḷ”. – car cārlas eliyaṭBuddhism and Hinduism’ vol 2; p.9

“Were our knowledge less one-sided, we might see that it would be more correct to describe Indian religion as Dravidian religion stimulated and modified by the ideas of Aryan invaders”

“The Brahmans are an interesting social phenomenon without exact parallel elsewhere. They are not like the Catholic or Muslim clergy, a priesthood pledged to support certin doctrines, but an intellectual, hereditary aristocracy who clim to direct the thought of India, whatever forms it may take.” 

‘The guiding priciples of the Brahmins have alway been not so much that they have a particular creed to enforce, as that whatever the creed of India, they must be its ministers’ 

(ii) viṣṇu teṉṉāṭṭil tirāviṭar vaḻipaṭṭa oru teyvam. piṉṉar āriyar viṣṇuvit tam teyvamākavum ēṟṟuk koṇṭaṉar (jē. pirislaski 1934-35 The name of the God Visnu and the Krsna legend” Quarterly Journal of the Mythic Society; pp 39-48. 

(iii) rikvēta kālattilēyē āriyar paṇpāṭu ‘muḻu āriyat’ taṉmiyi iḻantuviṭṭatu eṉpati avvēta 8 ām maṇṭalatti liruntu uṉṉikkalām. āriyarallātār paṇpāṭu rikvētattil uḷḷatu - atuvum kuṟippākap perumaḷavukku eṭṭām maṇṭalattil. (kuññaṉ rājā 1940 kē.vi. raṅkacāmi aiyaṅkār pārāṭṭu malar)

The presence of the eighth book leads one to the hypothesis that even at the time of the Rig Veda, the Aryan culture had ceased to be purely an Aryan one. There is a good deal of non-Aryan element in the Rig Veda, mostly in the eighth book. 

(iv) iltikō pukās karuttu: rikvēta riṣikaḷil perum pālōr āriyarallātārākattāṉ iruntirukkavēṇṭum. South Asian Archelogy 1981 (Cambridge, 1984)

(v) ‘intiyāvil vētakālattukku muntiya kālac camayam perumaḷavukku hārappā nākarikattil vaḻaṅkiya tirāviṭacamaya mākavē iruntatu. accamayatti mutalil kaṭippiṭikkat toṭaṅ kiyavarkaḷ ‘rikvēta ’ āriyarukku muṉṉarē cintuppakutikku vantu viṭṭa āriyarkaḷ (piṟṟi ‘rikvēta ’ āriyar ivarkaḷi, tāsyu, virātya eṉa aḻittaṉar) anta ‘munti āriyar’ tākkattiṉāl ak koḷkirikvēta āriyariṭamum paravi av vētattilum ēṟiyatu’. “cāvittiri toṉmamum ataṉuṭiṉ toṭar puṭiya caṭaṅkukaḷum cintu nākarika kālattilēyē uruvāṉavi.” 	        - askō parpōlā

The religion of pre-Vedic India goes back to a large extent to a Dravidian sub-stratum (linked with the Harappan culture) whose tradiitons were transmitted to Vedic times by an earlier wave of non-Vedic Aryans, the Dasa and Vratyas: Asko Parpola. 1981 Studia Orientalia, 50)

There is new evidence for the Harappan and Dravidian origin of many  conceptions and cultic practices that are central in later Indian religions, not only in  Saivism and Sakti - Tantrism, but in the Vedic ritual too. Asko Parpola (1985). The sky garment: A study of the Harappan religion and and its relation to the Mesopotamian and later Indian religions’ Studia Orientalia 57.

“Savitri and Resurrection: the ideal of devoted wife, her forehead mark, Sati and human sacrifice in Epic - Puranic, Vedic, Harappan - Dravidian, and Near - Eastern perspectives”: Asko Parpola; Studia Orientalia (1998). 

(vi) 1985 il veḷivanta South Asian Ardreology 1983 nūlil ālciṉ karuttu “cintu nākarika mutunilik kālattilēyē (2900 - 1500). intō - āriya moḻi pēcunar iṅku vantu cintu camayam - paṇpāṭṭuk kūṟukaḷi uḷvāṅkik koṇṭu piṉṉar rikvētam iṟuti vaṭivam peṟṟa ki.mu.1000ttil cintuc camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷ rik vētattil ēṟiyirukkavēṇṭum eṉpatākum. 

(vii) intu camayattil yāṇṭum kāṇappaṭum uruva vaḻipāṭu intiyāvil irunta āriyarallātār vaḻipāṭṭu muṟikaḷil iruntē pirāmaṇīyattukku vantatu. (G.M. Bongard Levin: A complex study of Ancient India, 1986). 

(viii) mokañcotarō muttiriyil kāṇappaṭupavar pōṉṟa (āriyarkaḷukku muntiya intiya) yōkikaḷ vantēṟikaḷuṭiya moḻiyic cila kālattukkuḷ kaṟṟukkoṇṭu tamatu camaya maṟi poruṭkaḷi (vētap pāṭalkaḷi iyaṟṟiya) pirāmaṇarkaḷukkuk kaṟpikkat toṭaṅkiviṭṭaṉarō?” (vālparṭ: A New History of India; 1982)

(ix) āriya nāṭōṭikaḷiṉ muraṭṭup paḻakka vaḻakkaṅkaḷ, avarkaḷuṭiya kaṭumiyāṉa perumpōrkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟukkum ahimcikkum (uyirkaḷukku ūṟu ceyyāmi), neṭuntolivu ākiyāl yōkam, tāyt teyva vaḻipāṭu pōṉṟavaṟṟippōla ahimciyum āriyarkaḷukku muṟpaṭṭa (cintut tamiḻ) makkaḷiṭa miruntē avarkaḷ peṟṟatāka nām karutavēṇṭiyuḷḷatu. (vālparṭ: An Introduction to India; 1991)

(v) ār.ci. taṇṭā karuttuppaṭi piruku (cintavar), cukra, cyavaṉa, āṅkiras, mahitās, vaciṣṭar mutaliyavarkaḷ tirāviṭarē. intu matattil uḷḷa muṉivaḻipāṭu, peṇteyvavaḻipāṭu, pūci, kōyil, uruva vaḻipāṭu, tantiram (Tantrism)  mutaliyavi tirāviṭak kūṟukaḷē; eṉṟum pala paḻiya tirāviṭak kūṟukaḷ atarva vētattil uḷḷaṉa
eṉṟum avar karutukiṟār. mēlum avar vēta kāla āriyariṭam intiyāvil uruvākiya pirāmaṇa vakuppu pōṉṟu ēṉi nāṭukaḷil intō airōppiyariṭam uruvākavilli eṉpatiyum cuṭṭik kāṭṭukiṟār.

“The institution of Brahmins is unique to the Vedic Aryans. It has no parallels in pre-Christian Indo-European traditions. - R.C. Dhanda; p 166. 

14. iṉṟu ulakil uḷḷa camayaṅkaḷil ellām toṉmiyāṉatu civa vaḻipāṭē eṉṟu mokañcotarō akaḻvāyvu  paṟṟiya 1931 āṇṭu nūlilēyē mārṣal cāṟṟiṉār. aṇmikkāla varalāṟṟaṟiñar cilar karuttum ituvē: vālparṭ (1991) ‘civaṉ vaḻipāṭu vaṭa intiyāviviṭa teṉṉāṭṭiltāṉ makkaḷiṭam mutaṉmi peṟṟuḷḷatu teṉṉāṭṭut tirāviṭamakkaḷ āriyarkaḷukku neṭuṅkālattukku muṉṉarē aṅku nilipeṟṟu iruntamiyi itu kāṭṭuvatākalām. tāyt teyva vaḻipāṭum teṉṉāṭṭiltāṉ mikuntu kāṇappaṭukiṟatu.’

ji.em. rāparṭs:putiya peṅkuyiṉ ulaka varalāṟu (2007) ‘iṉṟu vaḻipaṭappaṭum intuk kaṭavuḷarkaḷil talimicciṟappu vāyntavar kaḷil oruvaṉ civaṉ. tolpaḻaṅkālattil iruntaviyum makkaḷ iṉapperukkam, avarkaḷ celvapperukkam ākiyavaṟṟi kuṟik kōḷākak koṇṭaviyumāṉa vaḻipāṭṭu muṟikaḷ pala civaṉ vaḻi pāṭṭil iṇintuḷḷaṉa. toṉmiyāṉa civaṉ vaṭivam pōṉṟa oṉṟi mokañcōtarō muttiri oṉṟu koṇṭuḷḷatu. civaṉ aṭiyāḷamākak koḷḷappaṭuvatum, taṟkālak kōyilkaḷil makkaḷ vaḻipaṭuvatumāṉa liṅkam pōṉṟa kaṟkaḷ hārappā nākarika eccaṅkaḷil uḷḷaṉa. eṉavē iṉṟu ulakattil niṉṟu nilavivarum camayaṅkaḷil civaṉ vaḻipāṭē aṉittiyumviṭat toṉmivāyntatu eṉa uyttuṇarac cāṉṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa.

15. tamiḻiyac (tirāviṭa) camayaṅkaḷ allāta ēṉiyaviyellām vilaṅkukaḷik koṉṟu kaṭavuḷarkkup paṭippatic ciṟanta vaḻi pāṭṭuc ceyalāka vitittatuṭaṉ maṉitaṉ uṇṇavum, kaṭavuḷarkkuk koṉṟu paṭikkavumē vilaṅkukaḷi avaravar kaṭavuḷ paṭittaṉar eṉat tamatu puṉita nūlkaḷil eḻuti vittuḷḷa niliyil, tirāviṭar camayaṅkaḷāṉa camaṇamum puttamumē kollā nōṉpi aṟivuṟuttiya māṇpuṭiyavi eṉṟu kaṇṭōm. ac camayaṅkaḷiṉ vērkaḷ cintu veḷi nākarikattil iruntirukkavēṇṭum.

16. cāṅkiyam āriyarallātāruṭiyatu; upaniṣattukkaḷ; cāṅkiyak karuttukaḷik kaṭaṉ koṇṭuḷḷaṉa; eṉpati mēlē kaṇṭōm. yōkamum āriyallātāriṭam (tirāviṭar - āstirik) uruvāṉa teṉpar ē.pi. karmarkar (Religions of India) ‘kuṭali’ eṉṉum colliliruntu kuṇṭāliṉi’ uruvākiyatu eṉpar avar. viviyaṉ vorttiṅṭaṉum (A History of Yoga; Routledge and Kegan Paul; 1982) ikkaruttiṉarēyāvar:

1922l car jāṉ mārṣal mokoñcotarōvil akaḻnta poḻutu yōkam paṟṟiya mikap paḻaṅkālac cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭittaṉa.

[pirāmaṇarkaḷiṉ vēta matattiṟku etirāṉavarkaḷum ataṟkup puṟampāṉavarkaḷum āṉa sramaṇa cuyacintaṉivātikaḷ] vēta matattiṟku muṟpaṭṭa camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷilum aṟṟi nāḷ ulakil paravalāka iruntu vanta kōṭpāṭukaḷāṉa vilaṅkukaḷi (ēṉ maṉitarkaḷiyum?) paliyiṭṭu kaṭavuḷarkaḷit tiruptippaṭuttutal, payirkaḷ nalla viḷiccal taruvataṟkāṉa caṭaṅkukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟukkum etirāṉavarkaḷāka iruntirukkavēṇṭum. ittakiya palavakippaṭṭa camayac cuyacintaṉi iyakkaṅkaḷil yōkap payiṟci potuvāṉa kūṟāka iruntatu.

cāṅkiya tattuvamum vētakālattiṟku muntiyē intiyāvil tōṉṟiyatākat terikiṟatu.

17. cintunākarika makkaḷip pūcāri aracarkaḷ Priest - kings āṇṭu vantaṉar eṉṟa mārṣal, vīlar ākiyōr karuttu ātāramaṟṟatu eṉpar pocel. (2002: 114-115) pūcāri - aracaṉ eṉa avarkaḷ cuṭṭum uruvam (DK 1909) pūcāriyumaṉṟu, aracaṉumaṉṟu, iraṇṭum kalantatu  malla; kaṟpaṉi uruvam eṉpār pocel.

18. piṟkāla intiya camūka - camaya amippiṉ mutaṉmik kūṟukaḷ palavum cintuveḷi nākarikattil kāṇappaṭṭaṉa eṉṟa vālparṭ (1991)ṉ kūṟṟu ivviyalukku muṭivuriyāka amiyum.

19. matam paṟṟik kūṟumpoḻutu iṉṉoṉṟiyum iṟutiyākak karuttiṟ koḷvatu nalam: palvēṟu camayaṅkaḷ, avaṟṟiṉ meyyiyal pōṉṟavi paṟṟiya uṇarvellām ekkālattilum ennāṭṭilumpotu makkaḷil pattuviḻukkāṭṭukkum kuṟivāṉavarkaḷiṭiyētāṉ ōraḷavukku iruntirukkum. ēṉiya 90 viḻukkāṭṭiṉar nilivēṟu. ivvuṇmiyi kaṉakacapip piḷḷi (1904) aṉṟē kūṟiṉār: ‘ellā nāṭukaḷilum, ellāk kālaṅkaḷilum (paṇṭum, iṉṟum) uḷḷatupōl tamiḻariṭiyēyum ittakiya camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷukkum vekumakkaḷ naṭimuṟiyil kaṭippiṭikkum camaya nampikkikaḷ, caṭaṅkukaḷukkum perutta vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu (As usual among all nations, ancient and modern, the philosophic doctrines of the Tamils were far apart from the popular beliefs and ceremonies). ‘muntu tamiḻc’ cintu nākarika makkaḷ camayattukkum itu poruntuvatutāṉ. itē karutti hec. ār. pēṭ tamatu tirunelvēli kecaṭṭiyaril (1917) piṉ varumāṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār:

ūrtōṟum potumakkaḷ maṉappūrvamāka ūkkattuṭaṉ vaḻipaṭum ērāḷamāṉa uḷḷūrt teyvaṅkaḷip paṟṟi aṭuttu viḷakkappaṭukiṟatu. mikapperumpāṉmiyāṉa makkaḷip poruttavarit taṅkaḷ aṉṟāṭa vāḻkki nikaḻccikaḷi nirṇayippavarkaḷum, (māntaṉ teyvamāka āṉāl eppaṭi naṭappāṉō appaṭi) ciṉam eṟpaṭṭāl kēṭuviḷikkak kūṭiyavarkaḷum, pontiki ēṟpaṭṭāl kēṭu varāmal taṭuttum, vantāl nīkkiyum nalam ceyyak kūṭiyavarkaḷum āka avarkaḷ nampuvatu civaṉ, tirumāli alla: palvēṟu uruvaṅkaḷil vaḻipaṭappaṭum tāyt teyvamākiya kāḷi, maṟṟum uḷḷūrt teyvaṅkaḷāṉa cuṭalimāṭaṉ maṟṟum atupōṉṟa ‘tuṭiyāṉa’ teyvaṅkaḷiyē

“The accounts which will shortly be given of the hosts of lesser deities and of the enthusiasm with which they are worshipped, may serve to show that the gods who for the mass of the people rule and direct their dily lives, who bring evil and can if willing remove it, who are intelligible beings - men on the grand scale are not Siva and Vishnu but Kali in her various forms, and (local gods like) Sudalimadan and his horrid crew.

iyal 7

cintu nākarikattukkum, elām, cumēriya,
ekiptu mutaliya nākarikaṅkaḷukkum uḷḷa
toṭarpupaṟṟiya hīrās (1953)
nūliṉ karuttukaḷ

potuvāka iṉṟu paṉṉāṭṭaṟiñar ēṟkum karuttu elām – cumēriya - ekiptu nākarikat tākkattāl tāṉ tamiḻiyac cintu nākarikamum uruvākiyirukkum; allatu cintu nākarikam uṭpaṭa aṉittumē ēṟattāḻa camakālattil ciṟappaṭintirukkum eṉpatutāṉ. cintu nākarikattiliruntu ēṉiyavi tōṉṟiyirukkalāmeṉṉum karutukōḷi eṇṇip pārppataṟku icivōrkūṭa oru cilarē; eṉiṉum akkarutukōḷi valuvāka muṉ vittu 1953l Studies in Proto Indo Mediterraneam Culture  eṉṉum nuṇmāṇuḻipulamikka āyvu nūli (551 pakkam) veḷiyiṭṭavar heṉṟi hīrās pātiriyār, speyiṉ nāṭṭic cērntavar. palvēṟu aṟiviyaṟ pulaṅkaḷil kaṭanta 60 āṇṭukaḷil nikaḻntuḷḷa vaḷarniliyiṉ aṭippaṭiyilum kūṭa annūṟ ceytikaḷiṟ cila āḻntu karutat takkaviyāy iruppatāl, avaṟṟiṉ curukkatti ivviyal tarukiṟatu. [ ] kuṟikaḷukkuḷ uḷḷavi maṭṭumē avar eḻutiyavi paṟṟiya cila karuttukaḷ.

(hīrās nūliṉ) aṟimukam 1 – 26

[intō āriya moḻi pēcunariṉ (ciṟueṇṇikkiyil) nuḻivukku muṉṉar] intiyāveṅkum tirāviṭamoḻi pēcunarē vacittu vantaṉar (heviṭ 1889; hāl 1913). rikvētam upaniṣattukkaḷ kālatti liruntē tirāviṭattākkam (viṉikkoḷki, maṟupiṟappu, yōkam) uḷḷatu. vēta, purāṇap pakutikaḷil pala paṇṭit tirāviṭa nūlkaḷiṉ moḻipeyarppukaḷē. [hīrācukku 22.11.1942 aṉṟu eḻutiya kaṭitattil vi.es. cuktaṅkar ‘yutiṣṭiraṉ kati āriyar varavukkum rikvēta ttukkum muntiyatu eṉpatu cariyāṉa karuttē’ eṉat terivittār.] nākarikamaṟṟa niliyil intiyāviṟkuḷ nuḻinta intō āriyar tamatu moḻiyi camskirutamāka (tiruntiya moḻiyāka) ākkikkoṇṭaṉar (They converted their rude matter - of - course speech -- a speech of shepherds and husbandmen - into a classical Sanskrit language)

tirāviṭamoḻi pēcunar naṇṇilakkari nāṭukaḷiliruntu intiyāviṟkum teṉintiyāviṟkum vantavarkaḷ allar. iṅkiruntu aṅku ceṉṟu piṉṉar ulakeṅkum nākarikattip parappiyavarkaḷ ivarkaḷē. ittuṟi āyvukaḷ niṟivaṭiyum poḻutu tirāviṭa nākarikamākiya cintunākarikam ulakanākarikattiṉ toṭṭil eṉpatu ēṟkappaṭum.

“the Dravidians of India, after a long period of development in this country, travelled westwards, and settling successively in the various lands, they found their way from Mesopotamia upto the British isles, spread their race – afterwards named Mediterranean owing to the place where they were known anthropologically -- through the west and made their civilization flourish in two continents, being thus the originators of the modern world civilization. The Mediterranean nations of the ancientworld were racial off-shoots of the mighty proto-Indian race”

[After the problems of decipherment of Indus script and those of the migration of Dravidian civilisation out of India to Elam, Sumeria and the west are solved.] India will be acknowledged as the cradle of human civilization.

iyal I: mokeñcotarō eḻutti vācittal 29-158.

cintu eḻuttiliruntu tāṉ pirami eḻuttu tōṉṟiyatu. atu karutteḻuttu ideo-phonographic; lipiyāṉatu Picto – Phonographic . avi aciyeḻuttu alla; meyyeḻuttu (maṭṭum) alla; muḻumiyāṉa coṟkaḷik kuṟippavi; vivaraṇikaḷ, kūṟṟukaḷ mutaliyavi ākum. cintu eḻuttuppoṟippukaḷip piṉvaruvaṉavaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭiyil paṭippatutāṉ poruttamākum:

(1) cintu nākarikam āriyaruṭiyatu aṉṟu; tirāviṭar uṭiyatu; (tirāviṭar naṇṇilakkari nākarikaṅkaḷiṉ makkaḷuṭaṉ toṭar puṭiyavarkaḷ. eṉavē cintup poṟippukaḷukkum cumēriyam, ekiptu, etruskaṉ, miṉōvaṉ mutaliya poṟippukaḷukkum cila oppumikaḷ irukkum); cintunākarika makkaḷ pēciyatu toltirāviṭa moḻi.

(2) (cintu nākarikat) tol tirāviṭamoḻiyiṉ ilakkaṇam vaḷarcciyaṭiyāta niliyil iruntirukkum.

(3) (i) muttirikaḷip patittup paṭikkum vācakaṅkaḷi valamiruntu iṭamāka L<--- R  paṭikkavēṇṭum. Symbol perumpālum iṭatu kōṭiyil īṟṟu vaṭivamāka varuvatu ‘atu’ (māṟaṉatu) eṉpatiṟ pōl uṭimip poruḷil, allatu cilavaṭivaṅkaḷil – valakkōṭiyil vantāl (cuṭṭup poruḷil).
